help disp
 DISP Display array.
    DISP(X) displays the array, without printing the array name.  In
    all other ways it's the same as leaving the semicolon off an
    expression except that empty arrays don't display.
 
    If X is a string, the text is displayed.
 
    See also <a href="matlab:help int2str">int2str</a>, <a href="matlab:help num2str">num2str</a>, <a href="matlab:help sprintf">sprintf</a>, <a href="matlab:help rats">rats</a>, <a href="matlab:help format">format</a>.

help display
 DISPLAY Display array.
    DISPLAY(X) is called for the object X when the semicolon is not used
    to terminate a statement. 
 
    For example,
      X = inline('sin(x)')
    calls DISPLAY(X) while
      X = inline('sin(x)');
    does not.
 
    A typical implementation of DISPLAY calls DISP to do most of the work
    and looks as follows.  Note that DISP does not display empty arrays.
 
       function display(X)
       if isequal(get(0,'FormatSpacing'),'compact')
          disp([inputname(1) ' =']);
          disp(X);
       else
          disp(' ');
          disp([inputname(1) ' =']);
          disp(' ');
          disp(X);
       end
    
    See also <a href="matlab:help inputname">inputname</a>, <a href="matlab:help disp">disp</a>, <a href="matlab:help evalc">evalc</a>.
